A pickup channel earning ₹10,800 crore would mean pickup earning more than all of Swiggy's delivery. That is not a market; it is an arithmetic artefact. The honest India TAM for Juicy's model, in platform revenue, is ₹100 to 1,200 crore a year, and ₹70 to 260 crore under a flat-fee model. For comparison, India's largest restaurant-software company, Petpooja, has raised $22 to 27 million in its life (sources differ), serves about 1 lakh restaurants, made ₹101 crore of revenue in FY25 and still lost money; the whole category raised $0.5 million in 2026 to date.

### 7.3 Demand side: students × meals × share pre-ordered

| Step                                                             | Conservative | Base          | Aggressive    | Status                                                |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | ------------- | ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| Regular, on-campus students out of 4.5 crore                     | 2.9 crore    | 3.4 crore     | 3.6 crore     | Assumption (distance and part-time learners excluded) |
| Share whose campus has a canteen that could run digital ordering | 25%          | 40%           | 60%           | Assumption                                            |
| Food purchases on campus per year                                | 80           | 100           | 150           | Assumption                                            |
| Average order                                                    | ₹100         | ₹120          | ₹150          | Assumption                                            |
| Addressable campus food spend                                    | ₹5,850 crore | ₹16,200 crore | ₹48,600 crore | Estimate                                              |
| Share pre-ordered via Juicy                                      | 3%           | 10%           | 25%           | Assumption                                            |
| Pickup order value                                               | ₹175 crore   | ₹1,620 crore  | ₹12,150 crore | Estimate                                              |
| Platform revenue at 10%                                          | ₹17.5 crore  | ₹162 crore    | ₹1,215 crore  | Estimate                                              |

Cross-check: the base campus figure (₹1,620 crore of pickup orders) would be about 2% of today's delivery market, plausible for a mature niche. The aggressive case (about 15% of delivery) is not credible for campus pickup alone.

### 7.4 Bengaluru

| Input                       | Value                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Status                               |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------ |
| Colleges in Bengaluru Urban | 1,148, the most of any district in India (Jaipur 742, Pune 517, Hyderabad 465)                                                                                                                                     | Fact, AISHE 2023-24                  |
| Students in Karnataka       | 27.6 lakh                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Fact, AISHE 2023-24                  |
| Students in Bengaluru Urban | Not published; estimated at 7 to 11 lakh                                                                                                                                                                           | Estimate                             |
| Tech workforce              | Sources conflict: 1.5 million "directly and indirectly" in Karnataka IT; 3.5 million IT/ITeS in Bengaluru per one Wikipedia figure; "over 1 million tech professionals" in a 2025 report. Used: 1.0 to 1.5 million | Estimate                             |
| Global capability centres   | 875 in 2025 (Hyderabad about 360)                                                                                                                                                                                  | Fact                                 |
| Named tech parks            | Embassy Manyata 100,000 to 150,000 workers; Global Village 20,000 to 25,000; Karle Town 30,000 to 40,000                                                                                                           | Secondary, unverified                |
| Restaurants in Bengaluru    | No authoritative count; Rapido Ownly onboarded about 20,000; a 2019 Zomato scrape had 51,700 listings with duplicates; BBHA claims 5,000-plus members. Used: 25,000 to 40,000 active delivery-listed restaurants   | Estimate                             |
| Bengaluru online delivery   | ₹9,000 to 13,000 crore of order value a year (11 to 15% of the national figure)                                                                                                                                    | Estimate                             |
| Household eating-out spend  | No Bengaluru figure. Nationally, urban households spend ₹6,996 a month per person with 11.1% on beverages, refreshments and processed food, about ₹776 per person                                                  | Fact (national); Bengaluru is higher |

Bengaluru TAM in platform revenue at a 10% commission:

| Segment                                                                                                 | Addressable spend (base assumptions) | Pre-ordered share, conservative / base / aggressive | Platform revenue                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Campuses: 9 lakh students × 40% with a digital-ready canteen × 100 purchases × ₹120                     | ₹432 crore                           | 3% / 10% / 25%                                      | ₹1.3 / 4.3 / 10.8 crore           |
| Offices: 1.2 million tech workers × 60% in parks with cafeterias × 220 days × 50% buying on site × ₹150 | ₹1,188 crore                         | 3% / 10% / 25%                                      | ₹3.6 / 11.9 / 29.7 crore          |
| Branded cafés and quick-service counters: about 2,700 outlets                                           | per-outlet table                     |                                                     | ₹5.8 / 30 / 162 crore             |
| Bengaluru TAM, platform revenue                                                                         |                                      |                                                     | about ₹11 / 46 / 200 crore a year |

- **Bengaluru SAM** (what Juicy as built can serve in three years with a two-to-three-person team: 50 to 150 campuses and 10 to 30 tech parks): about ₹10 to 60 crore a year of platform revenue.
- **Bengaluru SOM** (what it can realistically win): 100 to 300 live outlets at ₹0.2 to 1.1 lakh each is ₹0.2 to 3.3 crore a year.
- **The roadmap's own pilot scenarios** (3,000 and 10,000 orders a month at ₹200 and 10%) are ₹60,000 and ₹2 lakh of revenue a month, so ₹7 to 24 lakh a year. These are the numbers that matter for the next 24 months.

### 7.5 Other metros and the world

- **Eight metros together** (Delhi NCR, Mumbai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Chennai, Pune, Kolkata, Ahmedabad): roughly 6 to 8 times Bengaluru on population and outlet counts, so about ₹70 to 90 crore (conservative), ₹280 to 370 crore (base) or ₹1,200 to 1,600 crore (aggressive) of platform revenue a year; a serviceable slice of ₹60 to 500 crore.
- **Global:** not computed, on purpose. Abroad, order-ahead is a mature feature inside POS and delivery apps (Toast, Square, Olo, Uber Eats, DoorDash); Starbucks takes roughly three in ten orders through its app (secondary); card fees of 2 to 3% erase the zero-fee UPI advantage; and Juicy's engineering edge (cheap Android, flaky 4G) is an Indian edge. Global expansion is irrelevant to the next five years.

### 7.6 Summary

| Geography                         | TAM, platform revenue a year (conservative / base / aggressive) | SAM              | SOM (3 to 5 years) |
| --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------- | ------------------ |
| India, supply side, commission    | ₹130 / 1,210 / (10,800, not credible) crore                     |                  |                    |
| India, supply side, flat fee      | ₹72 / 264 / 648 crore                                           |                  |                    |
| India, demand side, campuses only | ₹17.5 / 162 / 1,215 crore                                       |                  |                    |
| Eight metros                      | ₹70-90 / 280-370 / 1,200-1,600 crore                            | ₹60 to 500 crore |                    |
| Bengaluru                         | ₹11 / 46 / 200 crore                                            | ₹10 to 60 crore  | ₹0.2 to 3.3 crore  |
| Global                            | not computed                                                    |                  |                    |

The one-line reading: at a 10% commission on ₹120 to 200 orders, Juicy's realistic market is a few hundred crore of platform revenue across all of India and a few crore in Bengaluru. That is a real niche, but one where ₹20,000 to 1.1 lakh of revenue per outlet per year has to carry sales, support and payment costs. The economics improve only if the per-outlet throughput is campus-canteen scale (1,000-plus transactions a day), or if Juicy becomes a feature of something bigger (a canteen contractor, a college system, a POS).

The independent pass (Part C), counting only the roughly 9,300 campuses with more than 1,000 students, got about ₹170 crore a year for India at a 10% commission (₹35 to 50 crore at 2 to 3%) and ₹1 to 6 crore for Bengaluru. Its campus-only figure almost matches this section's campus-only base case (₹162 crore). The gap between the headline numbers comes only from whether offices and cafés are counted, and Part D explains why the narrower number is the safer one to plan on.

## 8. Trends, rules and "why now"

### 8.1 Trends, each with a verdict for Juicy

**The incumbents' economics**

| Trend                                             | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Verdict                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Take rates keep rising                            | Swiggy 24.0% (FY22) to 26.0% (Q1 FY27); Eternal's adjusted revenue 32.8% of net order value versus about 29.6% a year earlier                                                                                                       | Helps: the restaurant's pain is growing and documented                                                          |
| Customer platform fees: ₹2 to ₹14.90 in 31 months | Zomato ₹2 (Aug 2023) to ₹14.90 (Mar 2026); Swiggy ₹17.58; now 4 to 5% of an order; ₹154 crore of Zomato revenue in one quarter. [Medianama, Mar 2026](https://www.medianama.com/2026/03/223-why-zomato-swiggy-hiked-platform-fees/) | Helps: the price gap between "app delivery" and "pay the café directly" widens every quarter                    |
| Food delivery is profitable and growing 18 to 20% | Swiggy ₹1,001 crore of food-delivery operating profit in FY26; Eternal ₹606 crore in one quarter                                                                                                                                    | Hurts: the incumbents have cash to defend any niche, and the delivery habit is strengthening                    |
| Advertising is a second take                      | Bengaluru restaurateurs report ad bills doubling from ₹9,000 to ₹18,000 a week; Eternal's consolidated ad spend ₹945 crore in a quarter                                                                                             | Helps: "no ads, no forced promotions" is a sellable promise                                                     |
| Loyalty and budget programmes deepen              | Zomato Gold's free-delivery floor cut to ₹99 and "meals under ₹250"; Swiggy One, 99-Store, the Toing budget app in 50 cities, "Desk Eats" for workplaces                                                                            | Hurts: the aggregators are explicitly chasing the ₹99 to 250 order and the office desk, Juicy's exact territory |
| Quick commerce has stopped eating food delivery   | Eternal: the effect "has now plateaued"                                                                                                                                                                                             | Neutral                                                                                                         |

**Restaurants and their software**

| Trend                                                           | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Verdict                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Digital tools are standard in organised restaurants             | Petpooja about 1 lakh outlets; three vendors hold about 85% of organised POS; DotPe sells QR ordering on a free plan plus 2 to 3%                                                                                                                             | Helps: a café owner already understands a tablet with an order queue                                                            |
| But restaurant software does not raise money                    | 652 startups, $66.7 million raised in total, $0.5 million in 2026 to date (Tracxn)                                                                                                                                                                            | Hurts: no investor has seen a venture-scale win from selling software to Indian restaurants                                     |
| Zero-commission channels are being built by well-funded players | Rapido's Ownly: citywide Bengaluru from 3 March 2026, zero commission, ₹30 flat delivery fee, about 20,000 restaurants, a magicpin partnership; Inc42 reports it "captured nearly 10%" of Bengaluru delivery (unverified). Eternal calls the impact "limited" | Hurts: the "cheaper channel" slot in Bengaluru is occupied by a funded player with a bike fleet; Juicy must win on pickup alone |
| Restaurant margins are thin and delivery-dependent              | NRAI: delivery was about 10% of a restaurant's business before COVID and is "the heart" now, about 50%; restaurateurs on aggregators report margins of 5 to 10%                                                                                               | Helps: the sales pitch writes itself: prepaid, no commission on walk-ins, no ads                                                |
| Food-tech consolidation and cold public markets                 | Curefoods shelved its IPO in June 2026 after funds rejected the valuation; Rebel Foods still loss-making; Swiggy bought Dineout; Eternal bought Blinkit and Paytm's ticketing                                                                                 | Mixed: fundraising is hard, but the incumbents are busy with quick commerce and going-out, not pickup                           |
| AI in restaurants, kiosks, voice and WhatsApp ordering          | Eternal is building an AI support product; no reliable Indian adoption data for kiosks or voice ordering was found                                                                                                                                            | Unverified                                                                                                                      |

**Consumers**

| Trend                                                  | Evidence                                                                                                                                      | Verdict                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Eating out is becoming more frequent                   | About 5 times a month today, 7 to 8 by 2030; India has a quarter of China's restaurants per million urban residents (Bain and Swiggy, 2024)   | Helps                                                                                                     |
| The online habit is three times bigger than in 2021    | 1 crore to 3 crore monthly online food users, FY21 to FY26; about 3.4 orders per user per month                                               | Mixed: people know how to order on a phone, but they expect it at the door                                |
| 10-minute delivery is expanding and retreating at once | Swiggy Bolt in 700-plus cities; SNACC shut; Blinkit Bistro adding kitchens at ₹50 to 150 price points; Zomato shut Quick and Everyday in 2025 | Mixed: it erodes "skip the wait" near home, but campus pickup is about the counter queue, not the courier |
| Affordability push                                     | ₹99 free-delivery floor, meals under ₹250, 99-Store, Toing                                                                                    | Hurts: the incumbents are funding the low-ticket orders Juicy's ₹120 to 200 campus orders compete with    |
| Cheap data and phones                                  | About 700 million smartphones; data at 0.65% of income; Android 94%                                                                           | Helps: a no-install web app on cheap Androids is viable                                                   |

**Payments**

| Fact                      | Value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| UPI, August 2026 (record) | 24.51 billion transactions, ₹29.82 lakh crore; 791 million a day; up 22% in a year. [Medianama](https://www.medianama.com/2026/09/223-upi-transactions-august-2026/)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Merchant payments         | About 63% of volume; more than 95% of merchant transactions are ₹2,000 or less                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| MDR from 15 October 2026  | Zero up to ₹2,000, now by government notification; 0.4% above ₹2,000, capped at ₹300; small merchants under ₹1 lakh a month on QR stay at zero; merchants may not surcharge customers; the Supreme Court declined to stay it. [Business Today, Sep 2026](https://www.businesstoday.in/personal-finance/story/upi-payments-to-remain-free-for-p2p-transactions-0-4-mdr-applicable-only-on-merchant-payments-above-rs2000-555715-2026-09-15) |
| Government incentive      | ₹1,500 crore a year paid to banks at 0.15% on small-ticket merchant UPI; nothing for platforms                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

Verdict: helps, with a catch. Juicy's ₹200 orders stay fee-free at the network level, and the ₹2,000 line is now law. But zero MDR is what the bank charges the merchant; the payment aggregator still charges its own 1.95 to 2% plus GST for collecting UPI, unless on a short promotion.

### 8.2 The rules Juicy must follow from day one

| Rule                                                                                         | What it says                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Effect on Juicy                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| RBI Payment Aggregator Directions, 15 September 2025                                         | Only RBI-authorised aggregators may collect money on merchants' behalf; merchant funds must sit in an escrow account at a bank; "a PA business shall not carry out marketplace business"; a marketplace may not accept payments for a seller the aggregator has not onboarded. [RBI Directions PDF](https://www.fidcindia.org.in/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/RBI-PAYMENT-AGGREGATORS-DIRECTIONS-15-09-25.pdf) | Juicy cannot hold customers' money itself. "Restaurant's share held until pickup" must run through an authorised aggregator's split or delayed-settlement product, with every café onboarded and KYC-checked as a merchant. Doable, but it adds friction per café and gives the aggregator, not Juicy, control of refund timing. The independent pass found a further hurdle: Razorpay's split product (Route) now requires more than ₹40 lakh of turnover shown in GST returns before a platform can use it, so a pre-revenue startup may not qualify ([Razorpay Route docs](https://razorpay.com/docs/payments/route/)) |
| GST section 9(5): the e-commerce operator pays the restaurant's GST                          | Since January 2022 the platform, not the restaurant, pays 5% GST on restaurant services supplied through it; from 22 September 2025 delivery services through a platform attract 18% GST. Eternal carries ₹420 crore of GST demands for 2019 to 2022                                                                                                                                                         | Juicy is almost certainly an "e-commerce operator" and would have to register and remit 5% on every café order from day one, a monthly compliance load for a two-person team. No delivery leg, so no 18% exposure. Whether a pickup-only platform where the café collects payment itself falls under 9(5) needs a tax adviser; no ruling was found either way. The independent pass adds the price consequence: if the canteen is not GST-registered and the platform must add 5%, the student pays ₹210 on Juicy against ₹200 at the counter, or the canteen absorbs it and a 10% commission costs it 15%                |
| DPDP Act 2023, rules notified 14 November 2025                                               | Full compliance by 13 May 2027: consent notices per purpose, breach notification, verifiable parental consent for under-18s; penalties up to ₹250 crore. [PIB note](https://static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2025/nov/doc20251117695301.pdf)                                                                                                                                           | Engineering time, and one real trap: first-year students can be 17, so a campus app needs an age gate or a parental-consent flow by May 2027                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Karnataka Platform-Based Gig Workers Act 2025 (in force 30 May 2025; rules 19 November 2025) | A welfare fee of 1 to 5% of each gig-worker payout; platforms are contesting it in the High Court                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Helps relative to delivery: Juicy has no riders, so none of this cost, while Ownly's and the aggregators' Bengaluru costs rise                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| FSSAI                                                                                        | Platforms must hold a licence and list only licensed food businesses (not re-verified for this report). FSSAI issued Swiggy a prohibition order over its Toing app's licence in July 2026, resolved in three days ([Mint, Jul 2026](https://www.livemint.com/companies/news/swiggy-fssai-prohibition-order-toing-app-licence-11783688463770.html)): a separate platform needs its own correct licence        | Collect each café's FSSAI number at onboarding; cheap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Consumer-protection e-commerce rules and dark-pattern guidelines                             | Cancellation, refund and grievance-officer duties for marketplaces (not re-verified)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | A grievance officer and a published refund policy are required; the roadmap's refund rules must match                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ONDC                                                                                         | Food fell from 76% to 20% of ONDC retail orders between 2023 and 2024; magicpin did 150,000 food orders a day in September 2024; no 2025-26 figures found                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Mixed: proves a 3 to 5% channel can exist, has not dented the duopoly; a pickup app could in principle publish its cafés to ONDC buyer apps for discovery                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

**Funding climate.** Indian startups raised $5.2 billion in the first half of 2026, down 9%; restaurant software raised $0.5 million in 2026 to date; Curefoods' IPO is shelved. [Inc42](https://inc42.com/features/indian-startup-funding-slips-9-to-5-2-bn-in-h1-2026/) Verdict: hurts. A pickup marketplace with ₹20 of revenue per order is not a 2026 venture story; it has to be bootstrapped, or sold as software.

### 8.3 Why now? 2026 against 2021

**What is genuinely better in 2026**

1. Zero-fee UPI under ₹2,000 is now statute, and the 0.4% fee above it (from 15 October 2026) makes the under-₹2,000 ticket the cheapest payment in India to accept. Juicy's ₹200 order sits squarely inside it.
2. The price gap customers see has widened every quarter. A Zomato order now carries a ₹14.90 platform fee plus GST, 18% GST on the delivery fee since September 2025, packaging, and restaurant price-loading to recover a 24 to 25% commission. None of this existed in 2021. "Pay the café price, nothing else" is a stronger message than it could have been.
3. Restaurant anger is organised and public: the July-August 2026 Bengaluru standoff, the NRAI case at the CCI, doubling ad bills. "Commission relief" is a live buying criterion.
4. Three times as many people transact online for food (1 crore to 3 crore monthly users), so the behaviour Juicy needs is already learned: browse a menu, pay by UPI, trust a status screen.
5. Cheap Androids plus a web app that installs to the home screen. In 2021 the default was "download the app".
6. The payment-aggregator rules are now clear, so a small platform can build on an authorised aggregator's escrow and split products instead of improvising.
7. Karnataka's gig-worker levy and the new labour codes raise the cost of every delivery order in Bengaluru, but not of a pickup order.

**What is worse in 2026 than in 2021**

1. The incumbents are profitable, enormous and already in the campus and office niche: Swiggy sells Desk Eats, a 99-Store and the Toing budget app; Zomato cut Gold's floor to ₹99 and curates meals under ₹250. In 2021 both were loss-making and distracted by the pandemic.
2. The "cheaper channel for restaurants" slot in Bengaluru is taken by Rapido's Ownly, launched citywide in March 2026 with 20,000 restaurants and a bike fleet.
3. Ten-minute delivery compresses the convenience gap near home and office.
4. Funding for food-tech and restaurant software is scarce, and public markets rejected a loss-making food IPO this year.
5. Compliance is heavier: DPDP by May 2027, GST remittance from the first order, aggregator onboarding per café. None of this bit a two-person team in 2021.
6. Restaurant costs are volatile (the 2026 commercial-LPG shortage drove a spike in cancellations); cafés under cost pressure sign fewer new tools.

**Net reading.** The 2026 case rests on three facts that were not true in 2021: zero-fee UPI under ₹2,000 is law; the aggregator order has grown a visible ₹30 to 50 of fees and taxes on top of the food; and restaurants are organised against the commission. It is weakened by three others: both incumbents are profitable and already selling into offices and budget cohorts; a funded zero-commission rival owns Juicy's city; capital for this category is thin. "Why now" therefore favours a narrow, cheap-to-run pickup utility sold to canteen operators and institutions, much more than a consumer marketplace that must win customers away from Swiggy and Zomato.

## 9. Business models, with the arithmetic

Three ways Juicy could make money, each followed from "sign an outlet" to "what is left". Researched facts are marked; everything else is an assumption that the pilot must replace with a measurement.

### 9.1 The cost inputs (researched, Bengaluru, 2026)

| Input                          | Figure                                                                                                                             | Source                              |
| ------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| Payment gateway on UPI         | 1.95 to 2% plus 18% GST, so about 2.3% of order value at list; 0% on short, capped promotions                                      | Cashfree and Razorpay pricing pages |
| Escrow / split settlement      | 0.2 to 0.25% plus GST (Cashfree Easy Split); 0.1% (Razorpay Route)                                                                 | Pricing pages                       |
| WhatsApp order-status messages | ₹0.136 to 0.145 each; two per order                                                                                                | Meta rate card via BSP pages        |
| Web push                       | Free                                                                                                                               |                                     |
| Hosting                        | Supabase Pro about ₹3,000 a month plus a small AWS server, ₹3,500 to 5,000; a load-tested set-up ₹13,000 to 26,000                 | Supabase and AWS pricing pages      |
| Field sales executive          | ₹19,000 to 35,000 a month gross (Glassdoor, Indeed, job ads); about ₹38,000 with on-costs                                          | Crowd-sourced; treat as a range     |
| Support executive              | ₹15,000 to 30,000 a month                                                                                                          | Job listings                        |
| Junior engineer                | About ₹37,500 a month (Glassdoor) to ₹1 lakh at product firms; ₹70,000 used                                                        | Glassdoor and salary guides         |
| Campus ambassador              | ₹3,000 to 15,000 a month                                                                                                           | Listings                            |
| Counter hardware, if supplied  | ₹12,500 to 27,500 (tablet plus thermal printer); ₹0 if staff use a phone                                                           | Flipkart listings, October 2026     |
| Small-business software churn  | About 4.1% of customers a month (39% a year) for US SMB software; 4 to 7% claimed for Indian micro-businesses (low-quality source) | CRV benchmark                       |
| Order frequency benchmarks     | HungerBox about 7.5 orders per registered user a month; Snackpass 4.5; Swiggy 3.4 to 3.9                                           | Company sites and filings           |

### 9.2 Model 1: commission on every order (the roadmap's model)

The chain, at the base-case assumptions (3 new outlets a month in year 1; 4% monthly outlet churn; each outlet ramps over six months to 500 orders a month at an average ₹180; an 8% commission, lowered from 10% because of the price anchors in section 5):

| Step                                                                                                  | Month 12                  | Month 24                  | Month 36                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| Outlets signed, cumulative                                                                            | 36                        | 108                       | 228                       |
| Outlets still active                                                                                  | 29                        | 76                        | 143                       |
| Orders a month                                                                                        | about 10,600              | about 30,100              | about 58,600              |
| Order value a month                                                                                   | ₹19 lakh                  | ₹54 lakh                  | ₹1.05 crore               |
| Revenue a month (8%)                                                                                  | ₹1.5 lakh                 | ₹4.3 lakh                 | ₹8.4 lakh                 |
| Direct costs (gateway 1.5% in year 1 then 2.3%, split 0.3%, refunds 0.3%, messages, hosting, support) | ₹0.7 lakh                 | ₹2.2 lakh                 | ₹4.3 lakh                 |
| Gross profit                                                                                          | ₹0.9 lakh (57%)           | ₹2.1 lakh (49%)           | ₹4.2 lakh (50%)           |
| Sales, marketing, salaries                                                                            | ₹2.8 lakh                 | ₹4.8 lakh                 | ₹6.3 lakh                 |
| Operating result                                                                                      | loss of ₹1.9 lakh a month | loss of ₹2.7 lakh a month | loss of ₹2.1 lakh a month |

Unit economics per outlet, year-2 inputs: cost to sign one outlet (CAC) about ₹10,600 (one executive at ₹38,000 signing 5 a month, plus ₹3,000 of onboarding); gross profit per active outlet about ₹3,600 a month; expected lifetime 25 months at 4% churn; lifetime value (LTV) about ₹90,000; LTV ÷ CAC about 8.5; payback about 3 months. Those look healthy, and they are the honest-looking part of the business. The catch is that only one in three signed outlets may ever activate (an assumption from the comparables), which triples the real CAC to about ₹32,000 and cuts LTV ÷ CAC to about 2.8 with a 9-month payback. And a 4% monthly churn means a third of the outlets signed this year are gone next year.

Customer side: cost per new ordering customer about ₹40 (a first-order free item, posters, ambassadors); contribution per order at 8% about ₹7; at 3 orders a month the customer pays back in about 2 months. Customer acquisition on a campus is cheap; keeping the habit through exams and vacations is the problem.

### 9.3 Model 2: a flat monthly fee, Juicy out of the money flow

The outlet pays ₹1,000 to 3,000 a month, collects payments itself on its own UPI QR (zero MDR, no aggregator contract, no escrow), and Juicy runs the ordering, the board and the notifications. Per outlet at ₹1,499 a month: direct costs fall to messages, hosting and support (about ₹1,200 a month at 500 orders), leaving a few hundred rupees of gross profit per outlet. The business only works if support is nearly free (self-serve, no phone line) and the fee is nearer ₹2,500. At 100 active outlets × ₹2,500 = ₹2.5 lakh a month of revenue against about ₹1.25 lakh of direct costs and about ₹2.2 lakh of team: still a loss. At 300 outlets it is a small profit. Churn is the killer: at 4% a month, 300 active outlets needs 12 new signings a month forever.

Advantages: predictable; matches how outlets already buy software; no per-order arguments at the counter; avoids most of the RBI, escrow and possibly GST complexity (section 8). Disadvantages: tiny per-outlet revenue; every sale needs a visit; no upside from volume.

### 9.4 Model 3: sell to the institution (the HungerBox and SpaceBasic model)

The college or park operator pays a campus fee (assumption ₹1 to 3 lakh a year, anchored on SpaceBasic's roughly ₹2.2 lakh per institution and HungerBox's management fees), every counter on campus goes live, vendors pay a small per-order fee (₹2 to 3) and the institution gets cashless control, queue data and transparency. At 30 campuses × ₹2 lakh = ₹60 lakh a year plus per-order fees, against a team of one salesperson, one engineer, part-time support and the two founders (about ₹30 to 40 lakh a year), plus gateway costs if Juicy is in the money flow. It roughly breaks even at 20 to 30 campuses and is genuinely profitable at 100. Sales cycles are months, procurement is slow, and HungerBox, SmartQ and SpaceBasic are the incumbents. But it is the only model in this category that has produced a profitable Indian company.

### 9.5 Model 4: the hybrid

A small campus fee from the college plus ₹2 to 3 per order from the outlet, and nothing from the student (founder decision 2). If the college pays about ₹2 lakh a year for about ten counters, each counter brings about ₹1,700 a month of campus fee plus ₹1,000 to 1,500 a month of order fees at 500 orders: about ₹2,700 to 3,200 per counter per month. That clears direct costs only if Juicy stays out of the money flow, with the outlet collecting on its own UPI QR. Inside the money flow, direct costs are about ₹3,585 a month at this volume. It is still the most plausible model on the evidence, and the one with the most invoices.

### 9.6 Summary of the key numbers (base case, commission model, year 2)

| Measure                                                    | Value                                                                    | Fact or assumption                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| CAC per signed outlet                                      | about ₹10,600                                                            | Assumption built on researched salary and onboarding costs                       |
| CAC per retained, active outlet                            | about ₹32,000                                                            | Assumption (one-in-three activation)                                             |
| LTV per active outlet                                      | about ₹90,000                                                            | Assumption (4% churn, 500 orders, 8%)                                            |
| LTV ÷ CAC                                                  | 8.5 on signed outlets; about 2.8 on active ones                          | Derived                                                                          |
| ARPU (revenue per active outlet per month)                 | about ₹7,200                                                             | Derived                                                                          |
| Outlet churn                                               | 4% a month (39% a year)                                                  | Researched benchmark for US small-business software; unverified for Indian cafés |
| Gross margin                                               | about 50% in the money flow (gateway and support); 80% or more out of it | Derived from researched gateway costs                                            |
| Contribution margin (after sales and marketing per outlet) | about 25 to 30%                                                          | Derived                                                                          |
| Payback                                                    | about 3 months per signed outlet; about 9 months per active one          | Derived                                                                          |
| Customer CAC                                               | about ₹40                                                                | Assumption                                                                       |
| Customer payback                                           | about 2 months at 3 orders a month                                       | Derived                                                                          |

## 10. Network effects and scalability: does Juicy get better as it grows?

A network effect means each new user makes the product better for everyone already on it. It is the thing investors most want to see, because it makes growth feed itself and makes copying hard. Here is an honest look at each kind.

| Kind of network effect                                                                                    | Does Juicy have it?                                                                                                                                                                            | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Customer → restaurant (more students make the app worth more to each canteen)                             | Yes, inside one campus, strongly. A canteen cares a lot whether 5% or 50% of its students are on the app.                                                                                      | Snackpass reached 80% of Yale in a semester once the first few outlets were on it; HungerBox users order about 7.5 times a month once the cafeteria runs on it                               |
| Restaurant → customer (more outlets make the app worth more to each student)                              | Yes, inside one campus; weak across a city. A student wants every counter on campus on one app with one wallet. A canteen in Yelahanka does nothing for a student in Jayanagar.                | Food network effects are local by nature; Ritual's 10,000 restaurants across 50 cities did not protect it                                                                                    |
| Data (the product improves with more orders: better prep-time estimates, demand forecasts, "your usuals") | Small but real. Prep-time prediction and peak forecasting get better per outlet with history, and the outlet benefits (less waste).                                                            | HungerBox sells "insights" to institutions; no evidence yet that this wins customers                                                                                                         |
| Geographic density                                                                                        | The only network effect the category has demonstrated. Value comes from owning most counters on one campus, then the next campus.                                                              | Tapingo: 5 campuses → 150; Grubhub adds about 60 campuses a year; Snackpass campus by campus                                                                                                 |
| Switching costs (how painful it is to leave)                                                              | Low on both sides today. A canteen's hardware is a phone; menus take an hour to re-enter. A student has nothing stored (no wallet, no points).                                                 | Stored value (meal dollars, wallet balance) and social features (gifting, points) were the only switching costs the comparables had. Juicy's roadmap has neither a wallet nor a social layer |
| Marketplace effects (does Juicy bring new demand to restaurants?)                                         | Mostly no. The students were already in the building. Juicy moves orders from the counter to the phone; it does not create many new ones, except at peak when the queue made people walk away. | This is why 10% is a hard sell: aggregators charge 25% for new demand                                                                                                                        |
| Viral growth (do users bring users?)                                                                      | Possible inside a campus: group orders, "order for the table", gifting. Nothing built yet.                                                                                                     | Snackpass' gifting and group discounts were its "central viral mechanism"                                                                                                                    |

**Does Juicy become more valuable as more restaurants and customers join?** Within one campus, yes: the tenth counter on the app makes the app a habit, and a habit makes the eleventh counter sign up. Across campuses, each new campus starts from zero. There is no evidence anywhere in this category of a city-wide or national network effect for pickup. Multi-homing is the norm: a campus café will keep its Swiggy and Zomato tablet and its Petpooja POS and add Juicy as one more order source.

### 10.1 The biggest scaling bottlenecks, in order

| Bottleneck                                  | Why it bites                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Restaurant acquisition is a field-sales job | Every comparable needed people walking into outlets. Thrive's founder: one to two years to educate a new market. HungerBox went from 126 to 270 clients in six years.                                                                                                      | A Bengaluru field executive costs ₹25,000 to 35,000 a month and might sign 8 to 12 outlets a month; at a one-in-three activation rate and ~39% annual churn, the cost per retained, active outlet is three to four times the cost per signed outlet |
| Demand is route-bound and seasonal          | Order-ahead is a habit tied to a daily route. Campuses empty for three to four months a year; offices emptied in 2020 and Ritual lost 70% of its traffic. HungerBox's daily orders fell 75% in the lockdown.                                                               | A single-campus business has a built-in summer                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Support scales with orders, not revenue     | At about ₹14.6 of contribution per order (see section 9), one ₹22,000-a-month support executive is paid for by about 1,500 orders a month. Prepaid orders generate refund and dispute messages that walk-up orders never did.                                              |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Payments                                    | The 0% UPI promotion the plan leans on (Cashfree) is capped at ₹20 lakh of cumulative order value and ends 31 March 2027; after that the gateway takes about 2.3% of every order including GST. The split-settlement product that escrow needs costs another 0.2 to 0.25%. | [Cashfree pricing](https://www.cashfree.com/docs/help/account/pricing); [Razorpay pricing](https://razorpay.com/pricing/)                                                                                                                           |
| Hardware and onboarding time                | ₹12,500 to 27,500 per counter if tablets are supplied; a staff phone costs nothing, which the current product assumes. Menu entry and photos take a visit.                                                                                                                 | Flipkart listings, October 2026                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Competition                                 | The giants can copy in weeks and have ₹2,000 crore-a-year marketing budgets (Eternal's FY25 advertising). Petpooja or DotPe can ship "prepaid pickup token" in one release and already reach 1 lakh outlets.                                                               |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Capital                                     | Snackpass needed about $96 million to reach $60 million of order value; Ritual $120 million; HungerBox $23 to 44 million (sources differ) and a decade to reach ₹9 crore of profit.                                                                                        | A bootstrapped version has to stay on one campus cluster for a long time                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Geography                                   | The category is conquered one campus or city at a time; nobody has shown a shortcut.                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

### 10.2 What this means

Juicy's growth engine, if it has one, is campus density: own most of the counters on one campus, make the app the default way to pay there, then repeat. That is a slow, sales-led, business-to-business motion, the same one HungerBox, Tapingo and Grubhub Campus run. It is not a viral consumer app, and the plan should not be funded or staffed as if it were.

## 11. Go-to-market: where to launch, with whom, and how to get the first 10, 100 and 1,000

"Launch everywhere" is how TinyOwl died. The evidence in sections 6, 10 and 16 says this category is won one campus at a time, so the plan below is deliberately small.

### 11.1 Where and with whom

| Decision                 | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| First city               | Bengaluru                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The brief targets it, and it has the most colleges of any district in India (1,148) and an estimated 0.7 to 1.1 million students; UPI and smartphone penetration are not a constraint. It is also where HungerBox, SmartQ, GoKhana and SpaceBasic are headquartered, so the big campuses are already taken and the long tail is what is left.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| First neighbourhood      | One cluster where three to five mid-size colleges (arts, science, commerce, management) sit within walking distance, none of them on a HungerBox, SmartQ or SpaceBasic client list, with canteens run by local contractors and cafés at the gates | Desk research cannot name it, because the deciding facts (which canteens are uncontracted, and where the queues are longest) are only visible on the ground. Dense clusters of older colleges worth surveying first include Malleshwaram-Seshadripuram and Basavanagudi-Jayanagar (an assumption from general knowledge of the city; check each college before approaching). Avoid PES, Reva, Manipal, NITTE and IIM Bangalore, which are named clients of SpaceBasic or SmartQ. Pick by measured queue, not by prestige. |
| First restaurant segment | Independent canteen contractors and campus-gate cafés with a visible peak queue, a menu that can be prepared ahead (tea, coffee, sandwiches, idli-vada, pre-plated meals, bakery) and an owner who is on site                                     | They feel the rush personally, decide in an afternoon, and can batch ahead. Avoid kitchens that cook to order on one tawa; order-ahead cannot help them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| First customer segment   | Day-scholar students and staff at those campuses who eat at the same counters most days (founder decision 3)                                                                                                                                      | The daily trigger exists (every college day); hostel students eat in the mess.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Second segment           | Owner-run cafés, tea stalls, bakeries, juice and snack shops within walking distance of the same college gates, with the same students as regulars                                                                                                | Same daily routine; they set their own prices; no institution contract covers them; usually the fastest yes. Offices and tech parks are dropped (founder decision 3)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

### 11.2 Getting the first 10 outlets

Founder-led, no salespeople. Walk in at 3 pm, after the rush, with a one-page offer and your own queue measurement of their counter ("I counted 22 people in your line at 12:40 yesterday, and 6 walked away"). Offer two months free, a printed QR stand, and a promise to be at the counter personally for the first week of rush. Ask for one thing in return: that staff call tokens from the board and keep a pickup corner. Expect to visit 30 to 40 outlets to sign 10. Cost: your time and about ₹5,000 of printing and stands.

### 11.3 Getting the first 100 outlets

Only after the first 10 show retention (section 12). One cluster at a time: three to five campuses, each with one paid student ambassador (₹3,000 to 15,000 a month) and one field executive (₹25,000 to 35,000 a month) covering the whole cluster. Three levers that the comparables used:

1. **Owner referrals.** A happy canteen owner introduces the neighbouring one. There is indirect evidence that word of mouth works in this trade: Thrive grew 7 to 8% a month in restaurants with no marketing budget, largely because restaurants pushed their own ordering links.
2. **POS integration.** Appear as an order source inside Petpooja (1 lakh outlets) or UrbanPiper so the outlet's existing billing screen shows Juicy orders and no new tablet is needed. This is engineering time, not marketing money.
3. **A measurable before-and-after.** Every pitch after the first ten should carry the pilot's numbers: peak orders per hour before and after, walk-aways before and after, no-shows.

At 8 to 12 signings a month per executive and a one-in-three activation rate, 100 active outlets is roughly 300 signed outlets and 12 to 18 months of one executive's work, or six months of three.

### 11.4 Getting to 1,000 outlets

This is where the model has to change, because nobody has reached 1,000 outlets in this category by walking into them one by one. Two routes, both business-to-business:

- **Institution contracts.** Sell the college administration or the canteen contractor a "campus ordering" package for every counter on campus, like HungerBox, SmartQ, SpaceBasic and Grubhub Campus do. Decision cycles are months, but one signature brings ten counters and a semester of default usage.
- **Channel partnerships.** Become the pickup module that a POS vendor (Petpooja, DotPe, SlickPOS) or a facility operator resells. The risk, seen with SmartQ and Compass, is that the partner buys you cheaply or builds it themselves.

Either route needs capital for a small sales team and 12 to 24 months. Without one of them, Juicy stays a 100-outlet business, which can still be a good small company (section 19, scenario A).

### 11.5 Getting the first 10,000 and 100,000 customers

- **10,000 customers** is one or two campuses at 30 to 40% penetration, or one cluster at 15%. Snackpass did 80% of Yale in a semester with flyers, a launch discount and gifting. Channels: QR at every counter, ambassadors at the gate and in the WhatsApp groups of each department, a first-order free item (an assumed ₹20 to 40 of cost per new user), orientation-week stalls. Budget (an assumption): ₹50,000 to 1 lakh per campus for the launch month.
- **100,000 customers** is 15 to 25 campuses. That is only reachable through the institution route in 11.4, because each campus launch repeats the whole playbook and the summer resets it.

### 11.6 Which channels are cheap and which are expensive

| Channel                                                    | Cost                                                                                                                                    | Verdict                                                                                                                                  |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| QR codes at the counter and on tables                      | Printing, tens of rupees per poster                                                                                                     | Cheapest; reaches people already in the queue, at the moment of pain                                                                     |
| Web push notifications                                     | ₹0 per message                                                                                                                          | Free; the product's first channel, correctly                                                                                             |
| WhatsApp order-status messages                             | ₹0.14 to 0.15 per message (Meta utility rate plus GST); two per order                                                                   | Cheap; free inside a 24-hour window the customer opened. [Meta WhatsApp pricing](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/whatsapp/pricing/) |
| WhatsApp marketing messages                                | ₹1.02 to 1.09 per message                                                                                                               | Expensive next to ₹14.6 of profit per order; ten promotional messages wipe out most of an order's margin                                 |
| SMS                                                        | ₹0.10 to 0.45 per message plus ₹5,900 DLT registration                                                                                  | Commodity; use only for OTPs                                                                                                             |
| Campus ambassadors                                         | ₹3,000 to 15,000 per student per month                                                                                                  | The channel every campus comparable used; the best evidence of effectiveness is Snackpass' penetration                                   |
| Instagram ads geofenced to a campus                        | ₹1 to 6 per click; ₹50 to 250 per thousand views                                                                                        | Cheap per click, but a campus audience is a few thousand people; frequency, not price, is the limit                                      |
| Referral credits                                           | ₹50 to 200 per referral (unverified industry norm)                                                                                      | Pays back in three to four orders if the referred user stays                                                                             |
| Free-food and discount launches                            | ₹20 to 40 per first order                                                                                                               | Effective (Snackpass, Grubhub+ Student, Zomato Gold) and expensive: one ₹40 discount costs nearly three orders of profit                 |
| Field sales to outlets                                     | ₹25,000 to 35,000 per executive per month, about ₹2,500 to 4,500 per signed outlet, three to four times that per retained active outlet | The only channel that produced restaurant supply in every comparable                                                                     |
| POS-vendor partnership                                     | Engineering time; revenue share unknown                                                                                                 | Puts Juicy on the outlet's existing screen; the cheapest path to hundreds of outlets if a vendor agrees                                  |
| Facility-operator partnership (Sodexo, Compass, HungerBox) | Enterprise sales time                                                                                                                   | They are competitors as much as channels: Compass bought SmartQ; HungerBox is entering universities itself                               |
| Offline (flyers, orientation stalls, canteen posters)      | Hundreds to a few thousand rupees per campus                                                                                            | Snackpass' first 100 users came from flyers                                                                                              |
| Influencers                                                | Not researched                                                                                                                          | Wrong tool: the audience is one campus, not a city                                                                                       |

Cheap per unit: QR, push, WhatsApp status messages, flyers, stipended ambassadors. Expensive relative to the margin: any funded discount above ₹15 an order, WhatsApp marketing at scale, paid app installs (₹15 to 60 per install before the person ever orders), and field sales measured per retained outlet. The comparables that reached 75 to 80% of a campus did it with either venture-funded discounts or an institutional contract that made the app the default. Neither is in Juicy's current plan.

## 12. Product-market fit: what to build, the "aha" moments, and how to measure

### The smallest product that can test the idea

The risky assumption is not "can we build ordering software" (that part exists). It is "will enough people at one outlet order ahead, again and again, and will the outlet pay for that". The MVP is whatever proves or disproves that at ONE outlet, in weeks, with real money.

| Bucket           | Feature                                                                                                                                                 | Why it sits here                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Must-have        | Menu with photos, prices and "sold out"; cart; checkout with name and phone                                                                             | Exists today. The order itself.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Must-have        | A way to pay that moves REAL money or a deliberate "pay at the counter" option                                                                          | Pretend payment proves nothing. If the aggregator paperwork takes weeks, launch with "pay at the counter when you collect" or the outlet's own UPI QR, and measure whether people prepay when offered. |
| Must-have        | Order number or token called out at the counter                                                                                                         | Exists. The pickup handshake.                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Must-have        | Staff order board with Preparing → Ready, and a "pause orders" switch for the rush                                                                      | Exists except pause. Without pause, the first big rush breaks trust.                                                                                                                                   |
| Must-have        | "Your order is ready" message that reaches a locked phone (web push, or a WhatsApp/SMS message typed by hand in the pilot)                              | Without it the customer must stare at the page, which is the queue again, only on a screen.                                                                                                            |
| Must-have        | A pickup time the customer can choose ("ready at 1:15")                                                                                                 | Students order in the lecture and walk over at the break. "As soon as possible" alone does not match how campus days work.                                                                             |
| Important        | Per-item stock and "sold out" on the live menu                                                                                                          | Exists. Prevents the worst experience: paying for food that is gone.                                                                                                                                   |
| Important        | Cancellation and refund rules that a 19-year-old can understand in one line                                                                             | Needed the day real money moves.                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Important        | Modifiers for Indian food ("less spicy", "no onion", "extra chutney")                                                                                   | A free-text note covers 80% at a canteen; structured modifiers come when the note field is abused.                                                                                                     |
| Important        | Simple outlet dashboard: today's orders, revenue, busiest hour                                                                                          | The one screen that makes an owner feel the tool pays for itself.                                                                                                                                      |
